# 3D Reconstruction Daily Papers

The project automatically fetches the latest arXiv papers for 3D reconstruction and 3D Gaussian Splatting keywords.

The subheadings represent the search keywords. For each keyword, the table keeps the most recently updated arXiv records returned by the API.

Click the repository's Watch button to receive notifications from the daily issue workflow.

Last update: 2026-10-31

## 3D reconstruction
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[LucidLyra: Feed-Forward Generative 3D Scene Reconstruction and Joint Understanding via Multi-Task Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40495v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present LucidLyra, a framework that makes generatively reconstructed 3D scenes directly queryable for semantics and instances in a feed-forward manner from video latents. While generative 3D reconstruction can synthesize virtual environments from a single image, downstream applications in robotics and physical AI require not only visual realism but also access to scene semantics and object identities. Existing 3D understanding approaches, however, require captured multi-view scenes, have limited generalization capabilities, or rely on expensive per-scene processing and optimization. To bridge this gap, our method distills complementary knowledge from a camera-controlled video diffusion model and frozen 2D vision foundation models into a unified model to jointly infer 3D Gaussians encoding geometry, appearance, open-vocabulary semantics, and class-agnostic instance cues. The resulting scenes support novel-view rendering, open-vocabulary segmentation, and object grouping without per-scene optimization, while training requires neither manual 3D annotations nor captured multi-view data. Our method outperforms baselines combining state-of-the-art 3DGS segmentation approaches with generative 3D scene reconstruction on curated synthetic and real-image-conditioned benchmarks, while preserving reconstruction quality. Furthermore, our latent formulation naturally extends to monocular video, reconstructing structured dynamic 3D scenes across time. By turning renderable but unstructured outputs into queryable 3D worlds, LucidLyra enables downstream applications including 3D scene editing, 3D asset mining, and robotic simulation.</p></details> | Project Page: https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/lucidlyra |
| **[MLLM-GS: Multimodal Large Language Model Prior-Guided Fast Novel View Synthesis in Extreme Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39936v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Novel view synthesis (NVS) in degraded scenes aims to reconstruct 3D scenes from multi-view observations affected by various degradations, and to generate clear and photorealistic images at unseen viewpoints. Despite recent advances, existing methods often rely on degradation-specific models, limiting their generalization across diverse extreme environments. Generative models provide rich visual priors, but their outputs often suffer from pixel misalignment and color deviations, which can propagate into the reconstructed 3D scene and compromise reconstruction quality. Furthermore, the high computational and memory costs of 3D reconstruction hinder the practical application of NVS in extreme environments. To this end, we propose MLLM-GS, a multimodal large language model (MLLM) prior-guided framework for fast NVS in extreme environments. MLLM-GS consists of two branches: MLLM prior-guided view enhancement and fast 3D scene reconstruction. The former leverages the rich visual priors of a frozen MLLM to enhance views under different severe degradations and introduces a lightweight correction network to reduce pixel shifts and color distortions in MLLM outputs. With supervision from only a few paired clean views, this branch produces reliable enhanced views for all degraded observations, providing pseudo-GT views for subsequent 3D reconstruction. The latter improves 3DGS efficiency by optimizing rasterization, Gaussian memory layout, and parameter updates, reducing redundant computation, memory-access overhead, and optimization costs. Extensive experiments on multiple degraded scenes demonstrate that MLLM-GS effectively handles various severe degradations and achieves high-quality NVS with significantly lower reconstruction time and memory consumption. Project Page: https://wzlwlm13.github.io/MLLM-GS</p></details> |  |
| **[GeoCond: A Conditioning-Aware Reliability Adapter for Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18465v2)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D foundation models such as VGGT predict cameras, depth, and point maps in a single pass, but can fail silently under low overlap, low parallax, and extreme relative rotation. Stratified analyses over these factors show that these failures are governed by geometric conditioning and are poorly captured by native aleatoric confidence. We introduce GeoCond, a lightweight reliability adapter for frozen feed-forward 3D backbones. GeoCond reads geometric conditioning cues and the backbone's own stability signals and outputs pose-level uncertainty and a refinement gate. During training, it can be supervised by frame-permutation orbit variance, ground-truth pose error when labels are available, or cycle residuals from unlabelled independent pose graphs. At inference, the default head requires only one backbone pass and a small MLP. On VGGT, GeoCond improves out-of-distribution (OOD) AUSE (area under the sparsification-error curve; lower is better) from $0.32$ to $0.20$ over native confidence on held-out outdoor scenes (and to $0.21$ when trained on indoor data only) and avoids the collapse caused by applying bundle adjustment uniformly, without improving on feed-forward poses there. For three of six backbones, cycle-distilled variants provide a ground-truth-free adaptation route, including cases where permutation variance vanishes on equivariant models. The same reliability signal supports gated refinement, pose-graph weighting, calibration, curation, and capture decisions. Reliable feed-forward 3D reconstruction requires not only predicting geometry, but also knowing when that geometry should be trusted.</p></details> | Accepted in WACV 2027 |
| **[Transformer-inspired architectures make machine-learned electron densities accurate and transferable enough to bypass self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39687v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce machine learning models that accurately predict the electron density, the central variable of density functional theory. They are built on Transformer-inspired architectures, which require little computation per parameter and can therefore be enlarged at low cost. The proposed models, SwinUNETR-V2 and its attention-free counterpart NeXtUNETR, achieve state-of-the-art accuracy on QM9, with normalized mean absolute errors in the density averaging 0.075% and 0.046%, respectively. The predicted densities are accurate enough to bypass the self-consistent iteration: a single Kohn-Sham step reproduces the self-consistent electronic structure. For NeXtUNETR, the median errors are 0.036 meV in the total energy, 0.65 meV/angstrom in the forces and 1.8 meV in the HOMO-LUMO gap. The forces not only recover the self-consistent minima in geometry optimization but also successfully drive ab initio molecular dynamics. Fine-tuned on small datasets, both networks also transfer accurately to systems containing elements absent from QM9 or larger than its molecules.</p></details> | 36 pages, 4 figures, 2 tables; Extended Data (4 figures, 2 tables) and Supplementary Information included |
| **[AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6$\times$ as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23$\times$ end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https://github.com/InfRec-Cardinal/AB-Attention.</p></details> | 20 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Distilling Large-Scale Machine Learning Interatomic Potentials to Predict Long-Timescale Electrolyte Transport Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39513v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurately predicting the transport properties of liquid electrolytes is critical for next-generation battery design and requires force fields that are both quantum-mechanically accurate and fast enough for long-timescale simulation. Recent large-scale pretrained machine learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) approach density-functional-theory accuracy, but remain too slow to access the necessary timescales to converge transport properties. We provide a knowledge distillation procedure to transfer the accuracy of a large-scale "teacher" MLIP into fast, system-specific "students", without any additional ab initio data. Our distilled models run ~40x faster, reproduce teacher-predicted structural observables, and produce stable 20 ns trajectories in days, rather than the months the teacher would require. Across a diverse set of sodium- and lithium-ion electrolytes, student-predicted self-diffusivities, ionic conductivities, and shear viscosities track experiment and outperform classical force-field predictions, establishing distillation as a practical route to accurate, long-timescale simulations necessary for high-throughput electrolyte screening.</p></details> |  |
| **[Challenge or Pass? A Dynamic Policy for MLB's Automated Ball-Strike System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39452v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Under Major League Baseball's automated ball-strike (ABS) challenge system, players must weigh the value of overturning a call against the risk of losing a challenge they may need later. We develop a policy that values an overturn through its effect on expected runs, accounting for the count, outs, and runners on base. Using pitch tracking and game records, we reconstruct challenge opportunities, including calls that players let stand. A Bayesian model represents players' imperfect impressions of pitch location and uses them to estimate overturn probabilities, with uncertainty calibrated from historical challenge decisions. Dynamic programming compares the expected gain with the future cost of losing a challenge, accounting for the stage of the game, the number of challenges remaining, and opportunities on both offense and defense. We develop the policy using games before the 2026 All-Star break and evaluate it without refitting on 983 games played afterward. Along the recorded sequence of opportunities, the policy recovers 0.536 modeled correction runs per game across both teams, compared with 0.452 under observed decisions, an improvement of 18.5%. The gain is 0.084 runs per game, with a 95% bootstrap interval of [0.065, 0.103]. The policy makes more challenges and recovers more value per successful review despite a lower overturn rate. These results show why overturn rate alone can be a misleading measure of challenge strategy.</p></details> | 22 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Pair-Dependent Judge Errors Change the Minimax Rate of Semantic Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39196v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Semantic entropy, the entropy of the meanings among a language model's sampled answers, is estimated from the verdicts of a judge that declares pairs of answers equivalent or not, and automatic judges err in ways that depend on the answers they compare. The existing minimax theory covers only judges whose false-equivalence probability is the same for all pairs. We model a known judge that declares two answers with meanings $a$ and $b$ equivalent with probability $q+ΔM_{ab}$, for a symmetric matrix $M$ and $q$ bounded away from $0$ and $1$, and show that for three meanings the rate is set by tree statistics on four answers, while on an open set of channels the consistency threshold is set by trees on five. For every channel in an explicit open class, the minimax squared risk is of order $N^{-1/3}$ with $N=n^4Δ^6$ on polynomial weak windows on which $n^5Δ^8$ exceeds a multiple of $\log n$, and of order $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ on every polynomial weak window when the tree functions on at most four vertices are injective. The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. For such a channel, on every polynomial weak window, the minimax risk is of the order of the sum of $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ and a term that decays exponentially in $n^5Δ^8$, so no estimator is consistent while $n^5Δ^8$ stays bounded, even as $N\to\infty$. For $Δ=n^{-β}$ with $β&gt;1/2$, channels of the class with injective four-vertex tree functions allow consistent estimation exactly for $β&lt;2/3$, and the channels of this open set exactly for $β&lt;5/8$, at which the risk exponent drops from $1/12$ to $0$. Computer-assisted certificates show that both kinds of channels occur on open sets.</p></details> | 47 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables. Code: https://github.com/yahiko-l/pair-dependent-semantic-entropy |
| **[Volley3D: Unified 3D Full-Sequence Reconstruction from Monocular Broadcasts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38358v1)** | 2026-10-28 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D reconstruction from single-view sports broadcast is a pivotal capability for automated analytics and immersive AR/VR replays, yet notoriously ill-posed due to difficult-to-calibrate moving cameras and depth ambiguity. Unlike widely studied racket sports, team sports such as volleyball feature unpredictable multi-contact sequences and high altitude jumps. These dynamics categorically invalidate the standard planar projection assumptions relied upon by existing methods. To resolve these limitations without 3D training supervision, we propose a complete, physics-grounded pipeline tailored to monocular broadcast volleyball. First, we include a correction module to the perspective drift induced by ground-plane projection by coupling temporal jump spotting with Newtonian interpolation for 3D player placement. Second, for 3D ball trajectory estimation, we design a dedicated sub-trajectory segmentation architecture alongside a collision-matching algorithm. By anchoring physical equations to reconstructed 3D hand vertices, we lift noisy ball 2D detections into a spatiotemporally coherent 3D scene. Evaluations on multi-camera benchmarks demonstrate our approach significantly reduces 3D spatial drift compared to 2D-reprojection baselines.</p></details> | 8 pages + 10 pages of supplementary. Videos available on the project webpage: noambsf.github.io/Volley3D |
| **[Aligning Misaligned Benchmarks for Single-View Novel View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38349v1)** | 2026-10-28 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>While single-view novel view synthesis methods have been dramatically enhanced recently, how to quantify their performance has yet to be standardized. In this work, we demonstrate that an important factor lies in how each method's own reference frame is aligned with the so-called ground-truth pose. Existing methods often rely on test-time optimization of target poses for this alignment, which inherently is ill-posed due to the complexity of the optimization landscape of camera poses vs render accuracy, leading to methods being evaluated at an arbitrary local optimum. Some recognize this limitation and thus rely on 3D reconstructions of entire scenes using training and test views to align poses, which may be infeasible. In this work, instead, we propose to use traditional 2D-to-2D and 2D-to-3D image matching techniques to align 3D reconstructions. We also evaluate only within co-visible regions, between different views, which is the only place where a `correct' ground truth can be defined. Our evaluations show that with proper coordinate alignment, novel-view synthesis methods that use explicit 3D Gaussians achieve the state of the art, despite being previously reported in the literature to be outperformed by pure image-based 2D methods. We further provide a simple baseline that directly utilizes an off-the-shelf video generator and a feed-forward geometry estimator. Our simple baseline is already comparable to state-of-the-art methods specifically trained for novel-view synthesis, without any task-specific training.</p></details> |  |
| **[Efficacy of Monocular Depth Estimation in Lunar Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38080v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent advances in monocular depth estimation have demonstrated highly accurate 3D reconstructions within in-distribution terrestrial domains. This study investigates the efficacy of several state-of-the-art approaches to reconstruct out-of-distribution lunar environments using the NASA Ames Polar Optical Lunar Analog Reconstruction (POLAR) stereo dataset and Apollo 17 surface imagery. By leveraging these datasets, we establish a robust analog-to-field validation pipeline that evaluates quantitative performance against ground-truth physical analogs and qualitative performance against historical field data. Our results indicate that select state-of-the-art techniques can successfully generalize to produce high-fidelity relative 3D reconstructions of the unstructured lunar surface.</p></details> | Accepted at the International Conference on Space Robotics (iSpaRo) 2026 |
| **[Honeycomb-Surfels: Compact Hexagonal Primitives for Efficient Scene Rendering and Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37668v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Novel view synthesis and 3D reconstruction have advanced rapidly with radiance fields and splatting, yet their memory and computational demands limit their use in resource-constrained settings. We introduce Hexels: novel planar hexagonal surface elements with a first-order spatial color field for efficient 3D scene and 2D image representation. Hexels exploit locally planar regions with smoothly varying appearance using fewer, larger primitives. Their hexagonal support provides an area- and boundary-efficient domain, while the local color field captures appearance variation within each primitive. Across 34 scenes, Hexels improve PSNR over memory-matched 2DGS by 4.53-7.38 dB across datasets while requiring lower training VRAM. We further ablate Hexels by showing that per primitive, Hexels outperform 2DGS by upto 4.74 dB while using lower training VRAM and having 2.31 times faster FPS. Even in unconstrained settings, Hexels offers complementary quality-memory trade-offs. For efficient 3D surface reconstruction, Hexels reduce error by 28.9% compared with memory-matched 2DGS across 15 DTU scans. On 6 Tanks and Temples surface scenes, Hexels achieves the highest mean F1 among compared methods (34.38%, versus 31.72% for 2DGS). We further also show that Hexels, as a general primitive, improves 2D image representation over Image-GS. Hexels push the Pareto frontier on the quality-efficiency trade-off of surface-aligned primitives.</p></details> | Project page at https://honeycomb-surfels.github.io/ |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. As modern applications transition to high-resolution imaging, there is a critical need for contour extraction that leverages multi-core CPU architectures without the complexity of tile stitching or heavy synchronization. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm designed for high-performance geometric boundary extraction. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, conducted on both consumer and server-grade CPUs, demonstrate that TRUCO significantly outperforms existing baselines. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[Learning to Code the World: Harness Evolution for Compositional 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37046v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing editable and compositional 3D scenes from a real image requires jointly reasoning about geometry, object structure, and spatial relationships. We introduce SceneEvolve, a framework that represents 3D scenes as executable code and improves reconstruction by evolving the harness around a small open-source agent. SceneEvolve combines SceneAct, a compositional semantic action interface built on parameterized primitives, hierarchical composition, and semantic spatial relations, with SceneHarness-RSI, which leverages a stronger teacher agent to iteratively evolve the harness using feedback from development-set reconstructions. Throughout harness evolution, the model parameters, action semantics, and execution backend remain fixed. The resulting harness provides a reusable reconstruction procedure that enables the small open-source model to reconstruct unseen scenes without parameter updates or teacher assistance. Across two in-domain datasets and one out-of-domain dataset, SceneEvolve with the comparatively compact Qwen3.8-27B model improves all four evaluated reconstruction metrics while reducing mean inference time by 47.0% relative to the base model. More notably, despite relying on a substantially smaller model, SceneEvolve achieves 32.3% higher reconstruction quality than the large proprietary GPT-5.6-Sol model, while reducing mean equivalent deployment cost by 59.8%. Furthermore, the harness evolved using Qwen3.8-27B also generalizes to Kimi-K3, achieving comparable reconstruction performance to GPT-6-Astra. The project page is https://haoyu-agent.github.io/SceneEvolve.</p></details> | Project page: https://haoyu-agent.github.io/SceneEvolve |
| **[GeoGSplat: Geometry-Grounded Non-Pixel-Aligned Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36760v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward Gaussian Splatting enables efficient novel view synthesis, but pixel-aligned prediction makes the primitive count grow with image resolution and view count, leading to substantial redundancy and increased computational cost. Non-pixel-aligned methods address this limitation by predicting a fixed number of Gaussians. Without explicit ray and depth constraints, these methods yield misplaced Gaussian centers, causing rendering artifacts under large viewpoint changes. To address this issue, we introduce GeoGSplat, a geometry-grounded feed-forward model that explicitly incorporates geometric priors into the non-pixel-aligned framework. A dual-branch encoder constructs Anchors and Triplane to capture coarse 3D structure and fine-grained scene features, respectively. Subsequently, a hierarchical decoder progressively decodes dense 3D points and Gaussian primitives, enabling 3D reconstruction and novel view synthesis. GeoGSplat surpasses both state-of-the-art pixel-aligned and non-pixel-aligned Gaussian Splatting methods on DTU and NRGBD in terms of novel view synthesis and reconstruction. The advantage of our geometry-grounded paradigm further increases as target viewpoints move farther from the input cameras, demonstrating stronger generalization to large viewpoint changes. Project Page: https://comedy0913.github.io/projects/geogsplat</p></details> |  |
| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. Event cameras report per-pixel brightness changes asynchronously at microsecond resolution, offering a complementary signal that is largely immune to these artifacts, but whether this modality can be incorporated into existing RGB based 4D reconstruction pipelines without a full architectural redesign remains an open question. In this preliminary study, we investigate a minimal extension: we augment Monst3R, an existing RGB-only 4D reconstruction model, with an additional event-camera input branch, leaving the rest of the architecture unchanged. Across 3 benchmark datasets, this simple RGB-event fusion reduces Abs Rel by 3.4% on average relative to the RGB-only Monst3R baseline, and a sensitivity analysis shows that the improvement grows with the magnitude of camera and object motion, with the largest gains observed under high object motion conditions where RGB-only reconstruction degrades most. These early results suggest that even a lightweight, non-invasive fusion of event data can meaningfully improve 4D reconstruction robustness, motivating a more thorough architectural investigation in future work.</p></details> | Accepted at NeVi 2026 workshop at the 19th European Conference on Computer Vision (ECCV 2026) |
| **[Tri-Directional Sub-Voxel Displacement and Ipsilateral Territory Decoupling for 3D Bilateral Coronary Artery Tree Reconstruction in Cardiac CT Angiography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05900v2)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Three-dimensional (3D) reconstruction of bilateral coronary trees (LCA and RCA) from cardiac computed tomography angiography (CCTA) is limited by $64^3$ quantization, territory ambiguity, and sinotubular junction (STJ) ostium drift. We present a canonical $SO(3)$ $(Z_{\text{IS}}, Y_{\text{PA}}, X_{\text{LR}})$ multi-scale representation and Riemannian arborescence framework. First, we map 3D CCTA volumes, centerlines, and thick-stem ostia into $(Z_{\text{IS}}, Y_{\text{PA}}, X_{\text{LR}})$ space, extending a 3D pyramid and tubular decoder with a $128^3 \to 64^3$ boundary skip, a stop-gradient $128^3$ residual branch, a sub-voxel displacement head $Δ\hat{\mathbf{u}}(\mathbf{x})$, a dual-channel ipsilateral head $(\mathbf{P}_{\text{LCA}}, \mathbf{P}_{\text{RCA}})$, and a $32^3$ ostium detector. Second, all $327,385$ active parameters ($398,843$ total) are trained in one 30-epoch AdamW loop ($ε= 10^{-8}$) under decoupled L2-SP and aortic-wall consistency. Third, our Riemannian solver couples cylindrical Valsalva-STJ calibration, $128^3$ orifice contact rooting, contralateral inhibition, centripetal pruning, and 4-stage curvature-adaptive normal-plane pullback. On 14 unseen ImageCAS development volumes (28 trees; cases 201--250 isolated), the framework achieves $2.1652 \pm 0.4686\text{ mm}$ $\text{CD}_{\text{CL}}$ ($\text{CD}_{\text{CL}}^{\text{arc1mm}} = 2.2697 \pm 0.4863\text{ mm}$, $p \le 0.00024$; $57.40\% / 78.20\%$ $\text{F1}@1/2\text{mm}$; $4.6880 / 4.9298\text{ mm}$ ostia). Zero-shot evaluation on 50 unseen volumes (Cases 251--300, 100 trees) yields $2.9389 \pm 1.2785\text{ mm}$ $\text{CD}_{\text{CL}}$ ($p \le 7.24 \times 10^{-7}$; ostia $5.1815 / 7.5442\text{ mm}$), with $2.7696 \pm 1.1919\text{ mm}$ pooled $N=64$ $\text{CD}_{\text{CL}}$ and $128/128$ trees ($β_0 = 1, β_1 = 0$, $0.0000\text{ mm}$ detachment).</p></details> | 8 pages, 2 figures, 4 tables |
| **[PIS3R: Very Large Parallax Image Stitching via Deep 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04236v4)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Image stitching aim to align two images taken from different viewpoints into one seamless, wider image. However, when the 3D scene contains depth variations and the camera baseline is significant, noticeable parallax occurs-meaning the relative positions of scene elements differ substantially between views. Most existing stitching methods struggle to handle such images with large parallax effectively. To address this challenge, in this paper, we propose an image stitching solution called PIS3R that is robust to very large parallax based on the novel concept of deep 3D reconstruction. First, we apply visual geometry grounded transformer to two input images with very large parallax to obtain both intrinsic and extrinsic parameters, as well as the dense 3D scene reconstruction. Subsequently, we reproject reconstructed dense point cloud onto a designated reference view using the recovered camera parameters, achieving pixel-wise alignment and generating an initial stitched image. Finally, to further address potential artifacts such as holes or noise in the initial stitching, we propose a point-conditioned image diffusion module to obtain the refined result.Compared with existing methods, our solution is very large parallax tolerant and also provides results that fully preserve the geometric integrity of all pixels in the 3D photogrammetric context, enabling direct applicability to downstream 3D vision tasks such as SfM. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed algorithm provides accurate stitching results for images with very large parallax, and outperforms the existing methods qualitatively and quantitatively.</p></details> |  |
| **[Ground Sample Distance Constrained k-Coverage Viewpoint Planning via Set Multi-Cover Formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36450v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Drone-based 3D reconstruction and inspection require viewpoints that satisfy image-quality constraints while observing each surface region multiple times, which overlap-based planning heuristics do not guarantee. We study ground sample distance (GSD)-constrained $k$-coverage viewpoint selection for structures represented by surface voxels. Candidate viewpoints are generated along outward surface directions, screened with distance, viewing-angle, field-of-view, and occlusion tests, and augmented for voxels with insufficient visibility. Selection is then posed as set multi-cover with per-voxel coverage demands. We propose a dynamically weighted greedy heuristic that prioritizes remaining coverage deficits and scarce viewing alternatives, and compare it with sequential overlap-based pruning and an integer linear programming (ILP) formulation on the same candidate pools. Across two structures, four voxel resolutions, and three coverage requirements, the proposed approach achieves full or near-full $k$-coverage with compact viewpoint sets, using only 3.00--16.39\% more viewpoints than ILP while offering an interpretable selection process based on coverage deficits and viewpoint availability.</p></details> |  |
| **[Any3R: Feedforward 3D Reconstruction from Any Camera Type](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36150v1)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Geometry foundation models recover camera poses and dense geometry from a set of images with a feed-forward network, but they expect pinhole images. That assumption is inside the backbone too: the positional encoding and the training loss both bind an image position to a direction in space, and both depend on the lens. We condition both on the dense ray field, the unit direction through every pixel, which describes any central camera without a lens type, distortion coefficients or a bound on field of view. Angular coordinates give the rotary encoding the measured ray angle of each patch, evaluated per view, and solid-angle weighting scales the point loss by the area each pixel covers on the sphere, so neither the encoding nor the supervision depends on the lens. Any3R is therefore a feed-forward model that takes any central camera, and is robust to mixing many camera types per scene, with no retraining per lens. On a benchmark of thirteen central cameras across three datasets, where every method reconstructs with its own predicted camera poses, it is the most consistently accurate method. Any3R enables scalable 3D reconstruction from heterogeneous crowdsourced and archival imagery collections sourced across diverse camera types. Project page: https://jingong-chen.github.io/Any3R/</p></details> |  |
| **[Does VGGT Know What It Does Not Know?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35806v1)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models such as VGGT regress cameras and geometry in a single forward pass, but they report no uncertainty for the camera poses they predict: an incorrect relative pose looks no different from a correct one. We ask whether the frozen model nonetheless knows when its pose is wrong. On HardMatch, a benchmark of 1,000 deliberately difficult image pairs on which VGGT fails on 35% of pairs, a linear probe on VGGT's frozen features predicts pose failure on held-out categories with ROC-AUC 93.5%, compared with 79.7% for VGGT's own point-map confidence and 77.7% for a classifier trained on all of VGGT's outputs. The probe catches failures VGGT is confident about, lets a downstream system abstain on the pairs it distrusts, and transfers without retraining to rotation failures on PhotoTourism and ScanNet-1500, beyond what co-visibility explains. | **[Transformer-inspired architectures make machine-learned electron densities accurate and transferable enough to bypass self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39687v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce machine learning models that accurately predict the electron density, the central variable of density functional theory. They are built on Transformer-inspired architectures, which require little computation per parameter and can therefore be enlarged at low cost. The proposed models, SwinUNETR-V2 and its attention-free counterpart NeXtUNETR, achieve state-of-the-art accuracy on QM9, with normalized mean absolute errors in the density averaging 0.075% and 0.046%, respectively. The predicted densities are accurate enough to bypass the self-consistent iteration: a single Kohn-Sham step reproduces the self-consistent electronic structure. For NeXtUNETR, the median errors are 0.036 meV in the total energy, 0.65 meV/angstrom in the forces and 1.8 meV in the HOMO-LUMO gap. The forces not only recover the self-consistent minima in geometry optimization but also successfully drive ab initio molecular dynamics. Fine-tuned on small datasets, both networks also transfer accurately to systems containing elements absent from QM9 or larger than its molecules.</p></details> | 36 pages, 4 figures, 2 tables; Extended Data (4 figures, 2 tables) and Supplementary Information included |
| **[AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6$\times$ as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23$\times$ end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https://github.com/InfRec-Cardinal/AB-Attention.</p></details> | 20 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables |

| **[Challenge or Pass? We develop a policy that values an overturn through its effect on expected runs, accounting for the count, outs, and runners on base. Using pitch tracking and game records, we reconstruct challenge opportunities, including calls that players let stand. A Bayesian model represents players' imperfect impressions of pitch location and uses them to estimate overturn probabilities, with uncertainty calibrated from historical challenge decisions. Dynamic programming compares the expected gain with the future cost of losing a challenge, accounting for the stage of the game, the number of challenges remaining, and opportunities on both offense and defense. We develop the policy using games before the 2026 All-Star break and evaluate it without refitting on 983 games played afterward. Along the recorded sequence of opportunities, the policy recovers 0.536 modeled correction runs per game across both teams, compared with 0.452 under observed decisions, an improvement of 18.5%. The gain is 0.084 runs per game, with a 95% bootstrap interval of [0.065, 0.103]. The policy makes more challenges and recovers more value per successful review despite a lower overturn rate. These results show why overturn rate alone can be a misleading measure of challenge strategy.</p></details> | 22 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Pair-Dependent Judge Errors Change the Minimax Rate of Semantic Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39196v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Semantic entropy, the entropy of the meanings among a language model's sampled answers, is estimated from the verdicts of a judge that declares pairs of answers equivalent or not, and automatic judges err in ways that depend on the answers they compare. We model a known judge that declares two answers with meanings $a$ and $b$ equivalent with probability $q+ΔM_{ab}$, for a symmetric matrix $M$ and $q$ bounded away from $0$ and $1$, and show that for three meanings the rate is set by tree statistics on four answers, while on an open set of channels the consistency threshold is set by trees on five. For every channel in an explicit open class, the minimax squared risk is of order $N^{-1/3}$ with $N=n^4Δ^6$ on polynomial weak windows on which $n^5Δ^8$ exceeds a multiple of $\log n$, and of order $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ on every polynomial weak window when the tree functions on at most four vertices are injective. The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. For such a channel, on every polynomial weak window, the minimax risk is of the order of the sum of $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ and a term that decays exponentially in $n^5Δ^8$, so no estimator is consistent while $n^5Δ^8$ stays bounded, even as $N\to\infty$. For $Δ=n^{-β}$ with $β&gt;1/2$, channels of the class with injective four-vertex tree functions allow consistent estimation exactly for $β&lt;2/3$, and the channels of this open set exactly for $β&lt;5/8$, at which the risk exponent drops from $1/12$ to $0$. | **[Multiplying matrices via polynomials: exposition and barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30054v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We give an exposition of OpenAI's recent proof that the exponent $ω_\mathbb{C}$ of matrix multiplication over the complex numbers satisfies $ω_\mathbb{C}\leq9/4$. Here we have $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)=2$ for $0\leq k\leq1/2$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)\leq1+k+1/(4k)$ for $k\geq1/2$, and, for real numbers $a\geq b\geq c\geq0$, $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)=a+b$ if $c(a+b)\leq ab$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)\leq(a+b+c)/2+(ab/c+ac/b+bc/a)/4$ otherwise. Our exposition is in the language of degeneration inequalities among polynomial multiplication tensors and Strassen's asymptotic spectrum duality. We moreover prove an operational barrier (which can be thought of as coming from an operational asymptotic spectrum) showing that the current analysis of all these upper bounds on $ω_\mathbb{F}$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$ is optimal and that in fact the current degeneration ingredients cannot improve the bounds.</p></details> |  |
| **[A spatiotemporal Bayesian hierarchical model of heat-related mortality in Catalonia, Spain (2012--2022): The role of environmental and socioeconomic modifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17148v3)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extreme heat represents a growing threat to public health as climate change intensifies. While its mortality impacts are well recognised, the interplay between heat, air pollution, and socioeconomic vulnerability remains insufficiently understood. We quantified the associations of extreme maximum temperatures and heatwaves with daily mortality in Catalonia (2012-2022) and evaluated how air pollutants-particularly ozone, nitrogen dioxide and particulate matter-and socioeconomic factors confound or modify this relationship. ## multi-view stereo
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Revisiting Multi-View Stereo: A Sequence-to-Sequence Formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24850v2)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Computing accurate geometry from multi-view images is a fundamental problem in computer vision. Recent feed-forward (FF) models jointly estimate 3D geometry and camera parameters, but they typically suffer from geometry distortion caused by reconstruction ambiguity, even when ground-truth camera parameters are supplied. In this paper, we study the multi-view stereo (MVS) problem with known camera parameters and propose a novel approach that bridges conventional MVS and FF methods. Rather than casting MVS as a sequence-to-one mapping that predicts depth only for a single reference view, we reformulate it as a sequence-to-sequence task, akin to FF models, that jointly predicts geometry for all input views. We introduce a global transformer-based architecture with two components that explicitly exploit camera-induced priors: ray-map embeddings that inject camera parameters into image patch tokens, making the transformer camera-aware, and a unified global cost volume that replaces conventional per-view cost volumes to jointly capture 3D structure across all views. Extensive experiments on multiple public benchmarks show our approach achieves state-of-the-art performance, surpassing both MVS and FF reconstruction baselines.</p></details> | Accepted to NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[Transformer-inspired architectures make machine-learned electron densities accurate and transferable enough to bypass self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39687v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce machine learning models that accurately predict the electron density, the central variable of density functional theory. They are built on Transformer-inspired architectures, which require little computation per parameter and can therefore be enlarged at low cost. The proposed models, SwinUNETR-V2 and its attention-free counterpart NeXtUNETR, achieve state-of-the-art accuracy on QM9, with normalized mean absolute errors in the density averaging 0.075% and 0.046%, respectively. The predicted densities are accurate enough to bypass the self-consistent iteration: a single Kohn-Sham step reproduces the self-consistent electronic structure. For NeXtUNETR, the median errors are 0.036 meV in the total energy, 0.65 meV/angstrom in the forces and 1.8 meV in the HOMO-LUMO gap. The forces not only recover the self-consistent minima in geometry optimization but also successfully drive ab initio molecular dynamics. Fine-tuned on small datasets, both networks also transfer accurately to systems containing elements absent from QM9 or larger than its molecules.</p></details> | 36 pages, 4 figures, 2 tables; Extended Data (4 figures, 2 tables) and Supplementary Information included |
| **[AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6$\times$ as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23$\times$ end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https://github.com/InfRec-Cardinal/AB-Attention.</p></details> | 20 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Distilling Large-Scale Machine Learning Interatomic Potentials to Predict Long-Timescale Electrolyte Transport Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39513v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurately predicting the transport properties of liquid electrolytes is critical for next-generation battery design and requires force fields that are both quantum-mechanically accurate and fast enough for long-timescale simulation. Recent large-scale pretrained machine learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) approach density-functional-theory accuracy, but remain too slow to access the necessary timescales to converge transport properties. We provide a knowledge distillation procedure to transfer the accuracy of a large-scale "teacher" MLIP into fast, system-specific "students", without any additional ab initio data. Our distilled models run ~40x faster, reproduce teacher-predicted structural observables, and produce stable 20 ns trajectories in days, rather than the months the teacher would require. Across a diverse set of sodium- and lithium-ion electrolytes, student-predicted self-diffusivities, ionic conductivities, and shear viscosities track experiment and outperform classical force-field predictions, establishing distillation as a practical route to accurate, long-timescale simulations necessary for high-throughput electrolyte screening.</p></details> |  |
| **[Challenge or Pass? A Dynamic Policy for MLB's Automated Ball-Strike System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39452v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Under Major League Baseball's automated ball-strike (ABS) challenge system, players must weigh the value of overturning a call against the risk of losing a challenge they may need later. We develop a policy that values an overturn through its effect on expected runs, accounting for the count, outs, and runners on base. Using pitch tracking and game records, we reconstruct challenge opportunities, including calls that players let stand. A Bayesian model represents players' imperfect impressions of pitch location and uses them to estimate overturn probabilities, with uncertainty calibrated from historical challenge decisions. Dynamic programming compares the expected gain with the future cost of losing a challenge, accounting for the stage of the game, the number of challenges remaining, and opportunities on both offense and defense. We develop the policy using games before the 2026 All-Star break and evaluate it without refitting on 983 games played afterward. Along the recorded sequence of opportunities, the policy recovers 0.536 modeled correction runs per game across both teams, compared with 0.452 under observed decisions, an improvement of 18.5%. The gain is 0.084 runs per game, with a 95% bootstrap interval of [0.065, 0.103]. The policy makes more challenges and recovers more value per successful review despite a lower overturn rate. These results show why overturn rate alone can be a misleading measure of challenge strategy.</p></details> | 22 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Pair-Dependent Judge Errors Change the Minimax Rate of Semantic Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39196v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Semantic entropy, the entropy of the meanings among a language model's sampled answers, is estimated from the verdicts of a judge that declares pairs of answers equivalent or not, and automatic judges err in ways that depend on the answers they compare. The existing minimax theory covers only judges whose false-equivalence probability is the same for all pairs. We model a known judge that declares two answers with meanings $a$ and $b$ equivalent with probability $q+ΔM_{ab}$, for a symmetric matrix $M$ and $q$ bounded away from $0$ and $1$, and show that for three meanings the rate is set by tree statistics on four answers, while on an open set of channels the consistency threshold is set by trees on five. For every channel in an explicit open class, the minimax squared risk is of order $N^{-1/3}$ with $N=n^4Δ^6$ on polynomial weak windows on which $n^5Δ^8$ exceeds a multiple of $\log n$, and of order $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ on every polynomial weak window when the tree functions on at most four vertices are injective. The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. For such a channel, on every polynomial weak window, the minimax risk is of the order of the sum of $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ and a term that decays exponentially in $n^5Δ^8$, so no estimator is consistent while $n^5Δ^8$ stays bounded, even as $N\to\infty$. For $Δ=n^{-β}$ with $β&gt;1/2$, channels of the class with injective four-vertex tree functions allow consistent estimation exactly for $β&lt;2/3$, and the channels of this open set exactly for $β&lt;5/8$, at which the risk exponent drops from $1/12$ to $0$. Computer-assisted certificates show that both kinds of channels occur on open sets.</p></details> | 47 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables. Code: https://github.com/yahiko-l/pair-dependent-semantic-entropy |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. As modern applications transition to high-resolution imaging, there is a critical need for contour extraction that leverages multi-core CPU architectures without the complexity of tile stitching or heavy synchronization. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm designed for high-performance geometric boundary extraction. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, conducted on both consumer and server-grade CPUs, demonstrate that TRUCO significantly outperforms existing baselines. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. Event cameras report per-pixel brightness changes asynchronously at microsecond resolution, offering a complementary signal that is largely immune to these artifacts, but whether this modality can be incorporated into existing RGB based 4D reconstruction pipelines without a full architectural redesign remains an open question. In this preliminary study, we investigate a minimal extension: we augment Monst3R, an existing RGB-only 4D reconstruction model, with an additional event-camera input branch, leaving the rest of the architecture unchanged. Across 3 benchmark datasets, this simple RGB-event fusion reduces Abs Rel by 3.4% on average relative to the RGB-only Monst3R baseline, and a sensitivity analysis shows that the improvement grows with the magnitude of camera and object motion, with the largest gains observed under high object motion conditions where RGB-only reconstruction degrades most. These early results suggest that even a lightweight, non-invasive fusion of event data can meaningfully improve 4D reconstruction robustness, motivating a more thorough architectural investigation in future work.</p></details> | Accepted at NeVi 2026 workshop at the 19th European Conference on Computer Vision (ECCV 2026) |
| **[Gaussians on Fire: High-Frequency Reconstruction of Flames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22459v3)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We propose a method to reconstruct dynamic fire in 3D from a limited set of camera views with a Gaussian-based spatiotemporal representation. Capturing and reconstructing fire and its dynamics is highly challenging due to its volatile nature, transparent quality, and multitude of high-frequency features. Despite these challenges, we aim to reconstruct fire from only three views, which consequently requires solving for under-constrained geometry. We solve this by separating the static background from the dynamic fire region by combining dense multi-view stereo images with monocular depth priors. The fire is initialized as a 3D flow field, obtained by fusing per-view dense optical flow projections. To capture the high-frequency features of fire, each 3D Gaussian encodes a lifetime and linear velocity to match the dense optical flow. To ensure sub-frame temporal alignment across cameras, we employ a custom hardware synchronization pattern -- allowing us to reconstruct fire with affordable commodity hardware. Our quantitative and qualitative validations across numerous reconstruction experiments demonstrate robust performance for diverse and challenging real and simulated fire scenarios.</p></details> | 38 pages, 17 figures, 8 tables; ECCV 2026 version with corrected results (bugfix in the initialization of our method for real-world scenes) |
| **[Cryptanalysis and Repair of "Decentralized Multi-authority ABE for NC$^1$ from BDH"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29224v2)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present a plaintext-recovery attack on both the DBDH-based construction and the CBDH-based variant of Datta, Komargodski, and Waters (Journal of Cryptology, 2023). The attack succeeds with probability one in the claimed static security model using one corrupt authority and two users, each unauthorized even when granted the corrupt authority's attribute. The flaw is an invalid extension of the LSSS matrix-normalization lemma originating with Rouselakis and Waters (FC,~2015): preserving complete-share distributions does not preserve coordinate-wise ciphertext masks, which a corrupt authority can expose to remove identity binding from other users' shares. For both variants, replacing these masks with independent full linear sharings of zero preserves correctness and restores ciphertext-distribution invariance. We prove static security of both repaired variants in the random oracle model under their respective BDH assumptions, using the original hardcore-bit convention for the CBDH variant. Both proofs allow adaptive identity-hash queries. The modification retains the original matrix-width and row-labeling restrictions, keys, decryption, and ciphertext size, requiring only additional encryption randomness and finite-field arithmetic. We also attack both multi-authority attribute-based inner-product functional encryption constructions of Datta and Pal (PKC, 2023) and establish correctness and matrix-substitution invariance of the same repair. We prove static security of the repaired small-universe construction under asymmetric DBDH. For the large-universe construction, we identify a missing source-group encoding in the original reduction and give a complete static security proof under an augmented $L$-DBDH assumption, whose generic bilinear group hardness we establish.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multiplying matrices via polynomials: exposition and barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30054v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We give an exposition of OpenAI's recent proof that the exponent $ω_\mathbb{C}$ of matrix multiplication over the complex numbers satisfies $ω_\mathbb{C}\leq9/4$. Our version of the proof gives $ω_\mathbb{F}\leq9/4$ over every field $\mathbb{F}$. We also treat the rectangular matrix multiplication exponent $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$. Here we have $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)=2$ for $0\leq k\leq1/2$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)\leq1+k+1/(4k)$ for $k\geq1/2$, and, for real numbers $a\geq b\geq c\geq0$, $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)=a+b$ if $c(a+b)\leq ab$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)\leq(a+b+c)/2+(ab/c+ac/b+bc/a)/4$ otherwise. Our exposition is in the language of degeneration inequalities among polynomial multiplication tensors and Strassen's asymptotic spectrum duality. We moreover prove an operational barrier (which can be thought of as coming from an operational asymptotic spectrum) showing that the current analysis of all these upper bounds on $ω_\mathbb{F}$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$ is optimal and that in fact the current degeneration ingredients cannot improve the bounds.</p></details> |  |

## structure from motion

| **[A Lightweight Fiducial-Based Pipeline for 3D Hyperspectral Mapping of ex-vivo Lumpectomy Specimens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14534v2)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Hyperspectral Imaging (HSI) is a promising modality for intraoperative assessment of resection margins in Breast-Conserving Surgery (BCS), but its clinical translation requires aligning the inherently 2D spectral information onto the 3D shape of the excised tissue so that suspicious regions can be precisely localized for targeted follow-up. We present a fully automated, calibration-free pipeline that produces a 3D hyperspectral point cloud of an ex-vivo lumpectomy specimen from a set of consumer-camera RGB images and a single top-down HSI acquisition. The 3D geometry is reconstructed with a deep-learning Structure-from-Motion backbone, stabilized in a metric reference frame by a custom bundle adjustment that enforces consistency on the corners of four ArUco markers placed around the specimen. | **[Metric Scale for Insta360 Structure-from-Motion from the Camera's Embedded IMU](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35554v2)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structure-from-motion (SfM) from the dual-fisheye video of a consumer 360-degree camera recovers geometry only up to a similarity transform; in the pipeline considered here, metric scale is obtained from smartphone ARKit or ARCore poses captured alongside the camera. | **[Transformer-inspired architectures make machine-learned electron densities accurate and transferable enough to bypass self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39687v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce machine learning models that accurately predict the electron density, the central variable of density functional theory. The predicted densities are accurate enough to bypass the self-consistent iteration: a single Kohn-Sham step reproduces the self-consistent electronic structure. | **[Challenge or Pass? Both proofs allow adaptive identity-hash queries. The modification retains the original matrix-width and row-labeling restrictions, keys, decryption, and ciphertext size, requiring only additional encryption randomness and finite-field arithmetic. We also attack both multi-authority attribute-based inner-product functional encryption constructions of Datta and Pal (PKC, 2023) and establish correctness and matrix-substitution invariance of the same repair. We prove static security of the repaired small-universe construction under asymmetric DBDH. For the large-universe construction, we identify a missing source-group encoding in the original reduction and give a complete static security proof under an augmented $L$-DBDH assumption, whose generic bilinear group hardness we establish.</p></details> |  |
| **[PLANA3R: Zero-shot Metric Planar 3D Reconstruction via Feed-Forward Planar Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18714v3)** | 2026-10-25 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper addresses metric 3D reconstruction of indoor scenes by exploiting their inherent geometric regularities with compact representations. Using planar 3D primitives - a well-suited representation for man-made environments - we introduce PLANA3R, a pose-free framework for metric Planar 3D Reconstruction from unposed two-view images. Our approach employs Vision Transformers to extract a set of sparse planar primitives, estimate relative camera poses, and supervise geometry learning via planar splatting, where gradients are propagated through high-resolution rendered depth and normal maps of primitives. Unlike prior feedforward methods that require 3D plane annotations during training, PLANA3R learns planar 3D structures without explicit plane supervision, enabling scalable training on large-scale stereo datasets using only depth and normal annotations. We validate PLANA3R on multiple indoor-scene datasets with metric supervision and demonstrate strong generalization to out-of-domain indoor environments across diverse tasks under metric evaluation protocols, including 3D surface reconstruction, depth estimation, and relative pose estimation. Furthermore, by formulating with planar 3D representation, our method emerges with the ability for accurate plane segmentation. The project page is available at https://lck666666.github.io/plana3r</p></details> | 39th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2025). The project page is available at: https://lck666666.github.io/plana3r |
| **[Compiling and forecasting implied volatility surfaces via survey sampling, tempered inverse-probability sequence learning, and causal activity clocks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33438v1)** | 2026-10-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Compiling implied volatility (IV) surfaces and forecasting derivative risk from high-frequency equity and options feeds requires addressing contract heterogeneity, schema shifts, volatility clustering, and non-stationary trading activity. Using a split-adjusted NVIDIA (NVDA) equity and options panel (2018-2025), this study integrates finite-population survey sampling, tempered inverse-probability sequence learning, and causal activity clocks. Four findings emerge. First, cross-sectional surface accuracy depends on matching the sampling design to the target functional: plug-in stratified allocations using lagged cell dispersion improve equal-weighted surface reconstruction with full grid coverage, whereas liquidity-weighted probability proportional to size (PPS) sampling excels on liquidity-weighted targets by construction yet inflates error when misapplied. Second, in longitudinal LSTM sequence training, PPS and Neyman designs retain more tail-volatility windows than simple random sampling (SRS); all three designs are equivalent to full-census training within chosen margins while cutting training time, though incremental forecasting gains of informative selection over SRS are not established. Third, out-of-sample evaluation shows a predictability contrast: short-horizon prices are not forecastable beyond a random walk with drift among tested models, whereas forward IV shows descriptive mean-reversion skill concentrated in a few post-shock reversal sessions (insignificant under session-clustered tests), with sequence models not beating a linear HAR-IV benchmark and feature pruning driving stability. Fourth, across a 6.3x start-to-end (nearly twentyfold trough-to-peak) price expansion, rolling volume and dollar clocks stabilize bar rates and compress intraday return kurtosis beyond diurnal scaling.</p></details> | 26 pages, 6 figures |
| **[H-2DGS: Height-Field Gaussian Splatting for Sharp and Deformable Neurosurgical Surface Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30910v1)** | 2026-10-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Neurosurgical navigation commonly presents magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) as 2D slices, making it difficult to relate the anatomy in those slices to the cortical surface observed through the surgical microscope. This requires the reconstructed cortex to form a clear boundary around the brain anatomy rather than a diffuse volume that obscures internal structures. Conventional 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) can reproduce observed views but, under limited surgical view coverage, may distribute opacity through a thick and geometrically ambiguous band. We introduce height-field 2D Gaussian Splatting (H-2DGS), a three-stage pipeline that concentrates a robust 3DGS reconstruction, fuses its depth into a shared truncated signed distance field (TSDF) surface, and represents that surface with curved, surface-oriented Gaussian primitives. Separate height, color, and opacity bases provide local geometry and appearance, while a neighbor-transition objective coordinates overlapping patches. The explicit surface further supports postoperative alignment: MRI-derived depth deforms the reconstruction, and tracked-color supervision with alternating optimization preserves its appearance. Across five real neurosurgical cases, H-2DGS gives low multi-view depth inconsistency, a compact 7.97MB representation, and interactive rendering at 29--42ms per frame. The resulting sharp, deformable surface integrates clearly with tractography and MRI anatomy, including a postoperative resection cavity.</p></details> |  |

## neural rendering
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
