| **[Transformer-inspired architectures make machine-learned electron densities accurate and transferable enough to bypass self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39687v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce machine learning models that accurately predict the electron density, the central variable of density functional theory. They are built on Transformer-inspired architectures, which require little computation per parameter and can therefore be enlarged at low cost. The proposed models, SwinUNETR-V2 and its attention-free counterpart NeXtUNETR, achieve state-of-the-art accuracy on QM9, with normalized mean absolute errors in the density averaging 0.075% and 0.046%, respectively. The predicted densities are accurate enough to bypass the self-consistent iteration: a single Kohn-Sham step reproduces the self-consistent electronic structure. For NeXtUNETR, the median errors are 0.036 meV in the total energy, 0.65 meV/angstrom in the forces and 1.8 meV in the HOMO-LUMO gap. The forces not only recover the self-consistent minima in geometry optimization but also successfully drive ab initio molecular dynamics. Fine-tuned on small datasets, both networks also transfer accurately to systems containing elements absent from QM9 or larger than its molecules.</p></details> | 36 pages, 4 figures, 2 tables; Extended Data (4 figures, 2 tables) and Supplementary Information included |
| **[AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6$\times$ as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23$\times$ end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https://github.com/InfRec-Cardinal/AB-Attention.</p></details> | 20 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Distilling Large-Scale Machine Learning Interatomic Potentials to Predict Long-Timescale Electrolyte Transport Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39513v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurately predicting the transport properties of liquid electrolytes is critical for next-generation battery design and requires force fields that are both quantum-mechanically accurate and fast enough for long-timescale simulation. Recent large-scale pretrained machine learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) approach density-functional-theory accuracy, but remain too slow to access the necessary timescales to converge transport properties. We provide a knowledge distillation procedure to transfer the accuracy of a large-scale "teacher" MLIP into fast, system-specific "students", without any additional ab initio data. Our distilled models run ~40x faster, reproduce teacher-predicted structural observables, and produce stable 20 ns trajectories in days, rather than the months the teacher would require. Across a diverse set of sodium- and lithium-ion electrolytes, student-predicted self-diffusivities, ionic conductivities, and shear viscosities track experiment and outperform classical force-field predictions, establishing distillation as a practical route to accurate, long-timescale simulations necessary for high-throughput electrolyte screening.</p></details> |  |
| **[Challenge or Pass? A Dynamic Policy for MLB's Automated Ball-Strike System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39452v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Under Major League Baseball's automated ball-strike (ABS) challenge system, players must weigh the value of overturning a call against the risk of losing a challenge they may need later. We develop a policy that values an overturn through its effect on expected runs, accounting for the count, outs, and runners on base. Using pitch tracking and game records, we reconstruct challenge opportunities, including calls that players let stand. A Bayesian model represents players' imperfect impressions of pitch location and uses them to estimate overturn probabilities, with uncertainty calibrated from historical challenge decisions. Dynamic programming compares the expected gain with the future cost of losing a challenge, accounting for the stage of the game, the number of challenges remaining, and opportunities on both offense and defense. We develop the policy using games before the 2026 All-Star break and evaluate it without refitting on 983 games played afterward. Along the recorded sequence of opportunities, the policy recovers 0.536 modeled correction runs per game across both teams, compared with 0.452 under observed decisions, an improvement of 18.5%. The gain is 0.084 runs per game, with a 95% bootstrap interval of [0.065, 0.103]. The policy makes more challenges and recovers more value per successful review despite a lower overturn rate. These results show why overturn rate alone can be a misleading measure of challenge strategy.</p></details> | 22 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Pair-Dependent Judge Errors Change the Minimax Rate of Semantic Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39196v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Semantic entropy, the entropy of the meanings among a language model's sampled answers, is estimated from the verdicts of a judge that declares pairs of answers equivalent or not, and automatic judges err in ways that depend on the answers they compare. The existing minimax theory covers only judges whose false-equivalence probability is the same for all pairs. We model a known judge that declares two answers with meanings $a$ and $b$ equivalent with probability $q+ΔM_{ab}$, for a symmetric matrix $M$ and $q$ bounded away from $0$ and $1$, and show that for three meanings the rate is set by tree statistics on four answers, while on an open set of channels the consistency threshold is set by trees on five. For every channel in an explicit open class, the minimax squared risk is of order $N^{-1/3}$ with $N=n^4Δ^6$ on polynomial weak windows on which $n^5Δ^8$ exceeds a multiple of $\log n$, and of order $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ on every polynomial weak window when the tree functions on at most four vertices are injective. The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. For such a channel, on every polynomial weak window, the minimax risk is of the order of the sum of $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ and a term that decays exponentially in $n^5Δ^8$, so no estimator is consistent while $n^5Δ^8$ stays bounded, even as $N\to\infty$. For $Δ=n^{-β}$ with $β&gt;1/2$, channels of the class with injective four-vertex tree functions allow consistent estimation exactly for $β&lt;2/3$, and the channels of this open set exactly for $β&lt;5/8$, at which the risk exponent drops from $1/12$ to $0$. Computer-assisted certificates show that both kinds of channels occur on open sets.</p></details> | 47 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables. Code: https://github.com/yahiko-l/pair-dependent-semantic-entropy |
| **[MeshSuite: A Unified Benchmark for Triangle- and Mesh-Based Neural Rendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01306v5)** | 2026-10-28 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Triangle- and mesh-based neural rendering seeks to bridge neural representations and graphics engines through triangle primitives compatible with standard rasterization. Yet existing methods were developed and evaluated under inconsistent settings, with few direct comparisons and little evaluation in graphics engines. We introduce MeshSuite, the first benchmark for systematic evaluation of engine deployment and Non-Engine Render in triangle- and mesh-based neural rendering. MeshSuite evaluates two deployment settings: Standard Deployment, using an opaque mesh pipeline with vertex colors and hardware Z-buffering; and Dedicated Deployment, using method-specific engine implementations to preserve appearance and compositing features. For mesh splatting, we also audit exported surfaces for topological and geometric integrity as downstream graphics assets. Our results show that engine deployment reduces image quality in both settings; among evaluated methods, MeshSplatting degrades least under Standard Deployment. Dedicated Deployment preserves most fidelity at roughly 5-33x the runtime of Standard Deployment across two deployment datasets. For mesh splatting, explicit connectivity and shared indexing remain limited: shared vertex indexing alone guarantees neither manifoldness nor global connectivity. These findings show that rasterizability is only part of graphics readiness and underscore the need to assess engine deployment. Project page: https://x-up-lab.github.io/research/meshsuite.</p></details> |  |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. As modern applications transition to high-resolution imaging, there is a critical need for contour extraction that leverages multi-core CPU architectures without the complexity of tile stitching or heavy synchronization. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm designed for high-performance geometric boundary extraction. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, conducted on both consumer and server-grade CPUs, demonstrate that TRUCO significantly outperforms existing baselines. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. In this preliminary study, we investigate a minimal extension: we augment Monst3R, an existing RGB-only 4D reconstruction model, with an additional event-camera input branch, leaving the rest of the architecture unchanged. Across 3 benchmark datasets, this simple RGB-event fusion reduces Abs Rel by 3.4% on average relative to the RGB-only Monst3R baseline, and a sensitivity analysis shows that the improvement grows with the magnitude of camera and object motion, with the largest gains observed under high object motion conditions where RGB-only reconstruction degrades most. These early results suggest that even a lightweight, non-invasive fusion of event data can meaningfully improve 4D reconstruction robustness, motivating a more thorough architectural investigation in future work.</p></details> | Accepted at NeVi 2026 workshop at the 19th European Conference on Computer Vision (ECCV 2026) |

The flaw is an invalid extension of the LSSS matrix-normalization lemma originating with Rouselakis and Waters (FC,~2015): preserving complete-share distributions does not preserve coordinate-wise ciphertext masks, which a corrupt authority can expose to remove identity binding from other users' shares. For both variants, replacing these masks with independent full linear sharings of zero preserves correctness and restores ciphertext-distribution invariance. We prove static security of both repaired variants in the random oracle model under their respective BDH assumptions, using the original hardcore-bit convention for the CBDH variant. Both proofs allow adaptive identity-hash queries. The modification retains the original matrix-width and row-labeling restrictions, keys, decryption, and ciphertext size, requiring only additional encryption randomness and finite-field arithmetic. We also attack both multi-authority attribute-based inner-product functional encryption constructions of Datta and Pal (PKC, 2023) and establish correctness and matrix-substitution invariance of the same repair. We prove static security of the repaired small-universe construction under asymmetric DBDH. For the large-universe construction, we identify a missing source-group encoding in the original reduction and give a complete static security proof under an augmented $L$-DBDH assumption, whose generic bilinear group hardness we establish.</p></details> |  |
| **[RigidDriveGS: Object-Centric Vehicle Reconstruction from Fixed Multi-Camera Drive-Through Captures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26638v2)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>High-fidelity 3D reconstruction of vehicle exteriors improves buyer confidence in online automotive marketplaces, but fixed multi-camera drive-through capture departs from standard Structure-from-Motion (SfM): static cameras observe a moving vehicle amid clutter, rotating wheels, fisheye distortion, and specular reflections. We present RigidDriveGS, an object-centric pipeline combining motion-gated tracking, bifurcated masks that exclude wheels for SfM while optionally retaining them for rendering, mask-guided dense correspondences on raw distorted images, rig-constrained global SfM, and clean object-centric neural rendering with trimap-aware opacity or density supervision and native-alpha white compositing. We evaluate 25 vehicles from 10 dealership installations and six phone-recorded vehicles, each captured from 14 independent handheld positions. Under a staggered per-camera temporal-block holdout, 3DGUT+MCMC achieves 30.42 dB PSNR, 0.90 SSIM, and 0.20 LPIPS, improving PSNR by 7.78 dB over undistorted 3D-GS; independently recorded reference LiDAR scans yield full/body Chamfer-L1 of 0.026/0.013 m and F1@5 cm of 0.703/0.885. These results support clutter-free, object-centric novel-view synthesis without controlled studio infrastructure.</p></details> | 8 pages, 6 figures, Accepted at IEEE/CVF WACV 2027 |
| **[Towards Effective and Transferable Physical Camouflage against Multi-View BEV-based 3D Perception in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32230v1)** | 2026-10-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Modern autonomous driving systems widely adopt multi-view Bird's-Eye-View (BEV) based 3D perception models due to their superior performance. Despite their success, the robustness of these models against adversarial camouflage attacks remains largely unexplored. Existing camouflage attacks primarily target single-view 2D detectors, limiting their effectiveness in multi-view 3D perception settings. To address this gap, we propose BEVCA, a novel adversarial camouflage generation framework tailored for multi-view BEV-based 3D perception. Our framework integrates a BEV-feature-based adversarial loss with a multi-view neural rendering module, enabling effective and transferable camouflage attacks across different models and tasks. Extensive experiments in both digital and physical settings demonstrate that BEVCA outperforms state-of-the-art baselines and exhibits strong robustness under real-world conditions. Our code is available at https://github.com/WAVE-AT-IDEA/BEVCA.</p></details> |  |
| **[RADIANT: Object-Centric Feed-Forward Global Illumination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32075v1)** | 2026-10-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward neural renderers synthesize global illumination across novel 3D scenes without per-scene optimization or runtime ray tracing. Existing transformer-based renderers tokenize individual triangles, so self-attention scales quadratically with mesh resolution. Yet macroscopic radiative transport is governed by the underlying surface geometry and object arrangements rather than a particular mesh discretization; finer tessellation can thus inflate token counts without changing the underlying transport physics, motivating a discretization-agnostic and object-centric representation. We introduce RADIANT (RADiance-based Inter-Actor Neural Transformer), an object-centric architecture for feed-forward global illumination from point inputs. By representing each object with a compact budget of 32 surface-patch tokens, RADIANT decouples attention sequence length from polygon counts. In a single network, an inter-object scene transformer models 3D radiative exchange and a view decoder renders images at target camera poses. To capture light transport without materializing O(N^2) bias matrices, invariant form-factor attention embeds geometric distances and surface orientations directly into query-key dot products as SE(3)-invariant inner products weighted by learned area and material gains. For primary visibility without mesh connectivity, camera rays cross-attend scene tokens guided by depth-tested point splats in a shared spherical frame. Across a 1.03M-scene benchmark under matched model capacity, RADIANT reaches 25 dB tone-mapped PSNR in 5x fewer training steps than RenderFormer, and achieves 32.2 dB at 600k steps (+3.3 dB over RenderFormer and +7.6 dB over RenderFormer-V2). We further demonstrate the scalability of RADIANT, which completes a 512x512 forward pass of a 500-object scene in 0.83 s, with computational cost scaling with the number of objects rather than polygons.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multiplying matrices via polynomials: exposition and barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30054v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We give an exposition of OpenAI's recent proof that the exponent $ω_\mathbb{C}$ of matrix multiplication over the complex numbers satisfies $ω_\mathbb{C}\leq9/4$. Our version of the proof gives $ω_\mathbb{F}\leq9/4$ over every field $\mathbb{F}$. We also treat the rectangular matrix multiplication exponent $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$. Here we have $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)=2$ for $0\leq k\leq1/2$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)\leq1+k+1/(4k)$ for $k\geq1/2$, and, for real numbers $a\geq b\geq c\geq0$, $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)=a+b$ if $c(a+b)\leq ab$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)\leq(a+b+c)/2+(ab/c+ac/b+bc/a)/4$ otherwise. Our exposition is in the language of degeneration inequalities among polynomial multiplication tensors and Strassen's asymptotic spectrum duality. We moreover prove an operational barrier (which can be thought of as coming from an operational asymptotic spectrum) showing that the current analysis of all these upper bounds on $ω_\mathbb{F}$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$ is optimal and that in fact the current degeneration ingredients cannot improve the bounds.</p></details> |  |

## novel view synthesis
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[MLLM-GS: Multimodal Large Language Model Prior-Guided Fast Novel View Synthesis in Extreme Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39936v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Novel view synthesis (NVS) in degraded scenes aims to reconstruct 3D scenes from multi-view observations affected by various degradations, and to generate clear and photorealistic images at unseen viewpoints. Despite recent advances, existing methods often rely on degradation-specific models, limiting their generalization across diverse extreme environments. Generative models provide rich visual priors, but their outputs often suffer from pixel misalignment and color deviations, which can propagate into the reconstructed 3D scene and compromise reconstruction quality. Furthermore, the high computational and memory costs of 3D reconstruction hinder the practical application of NVS in extreme environments. To this end, we propose MLLM-GS, a multimodal large language model (MLLM) prior-guided framework for fast NVS in extreme environments. MLLM-GS consists of two branches: MLLM prior-guided view enhancement and fast 3D scene reconstruction. The former leverages the rich visual priors of a frozen MLLM to enhance views under different severe degradations and introduces a lightweight correction network to reduce pixel shifts and color distortions in MLLM outputs. With supervision from only a few paired clean views, this branch produces reliable enhanced views for all degraded observations, providing pseudo-GT views for subsequent 3D reconstruction. The latter improves 3DGS efficiency by optimizing rasterization, Gaussian memory layout, and parameter updates, reducing redundant computation, memory-access overhead, and optimization costs. Extensive experiments on multiple degraded scenes demonstrate that MLLM-GS effectively handles various severe degradations and achieves high-quality NVS with significantly lower reconstruction time and memory consumption. Project Page: https://wzlwlm13.github.io/MLLM-GS</p></details> |  |

The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. ## 3D Gaussian Splatting
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[NRF-GS: Neural Residual Fields for Expressive and Compact Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37115v2)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We revisit the role of appearance modeling in 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) and show that limited expressiveness in view-dependent reflectance is a key driver of representation redundancy. Our key insight is that by accurately capturing high-frequency directional reflectance, especially in specular regions, the GS-representation becomes more expressive, reducing the need for geometrically redundant splats. As a result, NRF-GS achieves comparable or better rendering quality while reducing the number of Gaussians by up to 50\%, and produces visibly improved specular and high-frequency details.</p></details> | Accepted at NeurIPS 2026 |

Existing 3D understanding approaches, however, require captured multi-view scenes, have limited generalization capabilities, or rely on expensive per-scene processing and optimization. To bridge this gap, our method distills complementary knowledge from a camera-controlled video diffusion model and frozen 2D vision foundation models into a unified model to jointly infer 3D Gaussians encoding geometry, appearance, open-vocabulary semantics, and class-agnostic instance cues. The resulting scenes support novel-view rendering, open-vocabulary segmentation, and object grouping without per-scene optimization, while training requires neither manual 3D annotations nor captured multi-view data. Our method outperforms baselines combining state-of-the-art 3DGS segmentation approaches with generative 3D scene reconstruction on curated synthetic and real-image-conditioned benchmarks, while preserving reconstruction quality. Furthermore, our latent formulation naturally extends to monocular video, reconstructing structured dynamic 3D scenes across time. The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. For such a channel, on every polynomial weak window, the minimax risk is of the order of the sum of $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ and a term that decays exponentially in $n^5Δ^8$, so no estimator is consistent while $n^5Δ^8$ stays bounded, even as $N\to\infty$. ## Gaussian Splatting
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[NRF-GS: Neural Residual Fields for Expressive and Compact Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37115v2)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We revisit the role of appearance modeling in 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) and show that limited expressiveness in view-dependent reflectance is a key driver of representation redundancy. In standard 3DGS, low-order spherical harmonics (SH) are used, restricting the splats' ability to model high-frequency directional effects, which is typically compensated by increasing the number of splats. Each Gaussian encodes a compact set of appearance features and a lambertian base color, while a lightweight \emph{global scene-level MLP} predicts view-dependent residuals conditioned on viewing direction, distance, and per-splat features. This formulation enhances directional reflectance modeling by combining diffuse per-splat reflectance representations with a shared global function for high-frequency details, enabling both higher expressiveness and parameter sharing across splats. Our key insight is that by accurately capturing high-frequency directional reflectance, especially in specular regions, the GS-representation becomes more expressive, reducing the need for geometrically redundant splats. As a result, NRF-GS achieves comparable or better rendering quality while reducing the number of Gaussians by up to 50\%, and produces visibly improved specular and high-frequency details.</p></details> | Accepted at NeurIPS 2026 |

Our refiner optimizes the object-to-camera pose with combined photometric and geometric losses under a continuous rotation parametrization free of gradient singularities. On a household-object benchmark, NovaSplat surpasses all model-based methods. | **[Transformer-inspired architectures make machine-learned electron densities accurate and transferable enough to bypass self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39687v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce machine learning models that accurately predict the electron density, the central variable of density functional theory. The proposed models, SwinUNETR-V2 and its attention-free counterpart NeXtUNETR, achieve state-of-the-art accuracy on QM9, with normalized mean absolute errors in the density averaging 0.075% and 0.046%, respectively. The predicted densities are accurate enough to bypass the self-consistent iteration: a single Kohn-Sham step reproduces the self-consistent electronic structure. For NeXtUNETR, the median errors are 0.036 meV in the total energy, 0.65 meV/angstrom in the forces and 1.8 meV in the HOMO-LUMO gap. The forces not only recover the self-consistent minima in geometry optimization but also successfully drive ab initio molecular dynamics. Fine-tuned on small datasets, both networks also transfer accurately to systems containing elements absent from QM9 or larger than its molecules.</p></details> | 36 pages, 4 figures, 2 tables; Extended Data (4 figures, 2 tables) and Supplementary Information included |
| **[AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6$\times$ as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23$\times$ end-to-end speedup. Areas with higher income inequality and older population structures also showed elevated mortality. These findings indicate that heat indicators raise summer mortality in Catalonia even after environmental and socioeconomic adjustment, while compound ozone exposure and contextual vulnerability further shape risk. Adaptation strategies should therefore combine heat-health warnings with air quality management and equity-oriented interventions.</p></details> |  |

| **[Challenge or Pass? A Dynamic Policy for MLB's Automated Ball-Strike System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39452v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Under Major League Baseball's automated ball-strike (ABS) challenge system, players must weigh the value of overturning a call against the risk of losing a challenge they may need later. We develop a policy that values an overturn through its effect on expected runs, accounting for the count, outs, and runners on base. Using pitch tracking and game records, we reconstruct challenge opportunities, including calls that players let stand. A Bayesian model represents players' imperfect impressions of pitch location and uses them to estimate overturn probabilities, with uncertainty calibrated from historical challenge decisions. Dynamic programming compares the expected gain with the future cost of losing a challenge, accounting for the stage of the game, the number of challenges remaining, and opportunities on both offense and defense. We develop the policy using games before the 2026 All-Star break and evaluate it without refitting on 983 games played afterward. Along the recorded sequence of opportunities, the policy recovers 0.536 modeled correction runs per game across both teams, compared with 0.452 under observed decisions, an improvement of 18.5%. The gain is 0.084 runs per game, with a 95% bootstrap interval of [0.065, 0.103]. The policy makes more challenges and recovers more value per successful review despite a lower overturn rate. These results show why overturn rate alone can be a misleading measure of challenge strategy.</p></details> | 22 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Pair-Dependent Judge Errors Change the Minimax Rate of Semantic Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39196v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Semantic entropy, the entropy of the meanings among a language model's sampled answers, is estimated from the verdicts of a judge that declares pairs of answers equivalent or not, and automatic judges err in ways that depend on the answers they compare. We model a known judge that declares two answers with meanings $a$ and $b$ equivalent with probability $q+ΔM_{ab}$, for a symmetric matrix $M$ and $q$ bounded away from $0$ and $1$, and show that for three meanings the rate is set by tree statistics on four answers, while on an open set of channels the consistency threshold is set by trees on five. For every channel in an explicit open class, the minimax squared risk is of order $N^{-1/3}$ with $N=n^4Δ^6$ on polynomial weak windows on which $n^5Δ^8$ exceeds a multiple of $\log n$, and of order $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ on every polynomial weak window when the tree functions on at most four vertices are injective. The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. For such a channel, on every polynomial weak window, the minimax risk is of the order of the sum of $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ and a term that decays exponentially in $n^5Δ^8$, so no estimator is consistent while $n^5Δ^8$ stays bounded, even as $N\to\infty$. For $Δ=n^{-β}$ with $β&gt;1/2$, channels of the class with injective four-vertex tree functions allow consistent estimation exactly for $β&lt;2/3$, and the channels of this open set exactly for $β&lt;5/8$, at which the risk exponent drops from $1/12$ to $0$. It also outperforms fisheye rendering baselines within their own FoVs and achieves the highest mesh F-score on FIORD and the main ScanNet++ set. | **[BudSplat: Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting under a Rendering Budget](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38278v1)** | 2026-10-28 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting has recently emerged as a promising alternative to per-scene optimization, enabling fast novel view synthesis by predicting Gaussian primitives directly from input images. The second is quality-aware Gaussian pruning that selects a compact set of primitives by jointly considering rendering contribution and detail importance. The quality-aware pruning enables local quota redistribution that helps avoid over-allocating primitives to large redundant regions. Experiments on RE10K, DL3DV, and ACID show that BudSplat achieves a favorable trade-off among rendering quality and efficiency.</p></details> |  |

| **[A spatiotemporal Bayesian hierarchical model of heat-related mortality in Catalonia, Spain (2012--2022): The role of environmental and socioeconomic modifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17148v3)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extreme heat represents a growing threat to public health as climate change intensifies. | **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[Transformer-inspired architectures make machine-learned electron densities accurate and transferable enough to bypass self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39687v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce machine learning models that accurately predict the electron density, the central variable of density functional theory. They are built on Transformer-inspired architectures, which require little computation per parameter and can therefore be enlarged at low cost. The proposed models, SwinUNETR-V2 and its attention-free counterpart NeXtUNETR, achieve state-of-the-art accuracy on QM9, with normalized mean absolute errors in the density averaging 0.075% and 0.046%, respectively. The predicted densities are accurate enough to bypass the self-consistent iteration: a single Kohn-Sham step reproduces the self-consistent electronic structure. For NeXtUNETR, the median errors are 0.036 meV in the total energy, 0.65 meV/angstrom in the forces and 1.8 meV in the HOMO-LUMO gap. The forces not only recover the self-consistent minima in geometry optimization but also successfully drive ab initio molecular dynamics. Fine-tuned on small datasets, both networks also transfer accurately to systems containing elements absent from QM9 or larger than its molecules.</p></details> | 36 pages, 4 figures, 2 tables; Extended Data (4 figures, 2 tables) and Supplementary Information included |
| **[AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6$\times$ as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23$\times$ end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https://github.com/InfRec-Cardinal/AB-Attention.</p></details> | 20 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Distilling Large-Scale Machine Learning Interatomic Potentials to Predict Long-Timescale Electrolyte Transport Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39513v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurately predicting the transport properties of liquid electrolytes is critical for next-generation battery design and requires force fields that are both quantum-mechanically accurate and fast enough for long-timescale simulation. Recent large-scale pretrained machine learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) approach density-functional-theory accuracy, but remain too slow to access the necessary timescales to converge transport properties. We provide a knowledge distillation procedure to transfer the accuracy of a large-scale "teacher" MLIP into fast, system-specific "students", without any additional ab initio data. Our distilled models run ~40x faster, reproduce teacher-predicted structural observables, and produce stable 20 ns trajectories in days, rather than the months the teacher would require. Across a diverse set of sodium- and lithium-ion electrolytes, student-predicted self-diffusivities, ionic conductivities, and shear viscosities track experiment and outperform classical force-field predictions, establishing distillation as a practical route to accurate, long-timescale simulations necessary for high-throughput electrolyte screening.</p></details> |  |
| **[Challenge or Pass? A Dynamic Policy for MLB's Automated Ball-Strike System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39452v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Under Major League Baseball's automated ball-strike (ABS) challenge system, players must weigh the value of overturning a call against the risk of losing a challenge they may need later. We develop a policy that values an overturn through its effect on expected runs, accounting for the count, outs, and runners on base. Using pitch tracking and game records, we reconstruct challenge opportunities, including calls that players let stand. A Bayesian model represents players' imperfect impressions of pitch location and uses them to estimate overturn probabilities, with uncertainty calibrated from historical challenge decisions. Dynamic programming compares the expected gain with the future cost of losing a challenge, accounting for the stage of the game, the number of challenges remaining, and opportunities on both offense and defense. We develop the policy using games before the 2026 All-Star break and evaluate it without refitting on 983 games played afterward. Along the recorded sequence of opportunities, the policy recovers 0.536 modeled correction runs per game across both teams, compared with 0.452 under observed decisions, an improvement of 18.5%. The gain is 0.084 runs per game, with a 95% bootstrap interval of [0.065, 0.103]. The policy makes more challenges and recovers more value per successful review despite a lower overturn rate. These results show why overturn rate alone can be a misleading measure of challenge strategy.</p></details> | 22 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Pair-Dependent Judge Errors Change the Minimax Rate of Semantic Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39196v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Semantic entropy, the entropy of the meanings among a language model's sampled answers, is estimated from the verdicts of a judge that declares pairs of answers equivalent or not, and automatic judges err in ways that depend on the answers they compare. The existing minimax theory covers only judges whose false-equivalence probability is the same for all pairs. We model a known judge that declares two answers with meanings $a$ and $b$ equivalent with probability $q+ΔM_{ab}$, for a symmetric matrix $M$ and $q$ bounded away from $0$ and $1$, and show that for three meanings the rate is set by tree statistics on four answers, while on an open set of channels the consistency threshold is set by trees on five. For every channel in an explicit open class, the minimax squared risk is of order $N^{-1/3}$ with $N=n^4Δ^6$ on polynomial weak windows on which $n^5Δ^8$ exceeds a multiple of $\log n$, and of order $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ on every polynomial weak window when the tree functions on at most four vertices are injective. The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. For such a channel, on every polynomial weak window, the minimax risk is of the order of the sum of $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ and a term that decays exponentially in $n^5Δ^8$, so no estimator is consistent while $n^5Δ^8$ stays bounded, even as $N\to\infty$. For $Δ=n^{-β}$ with $β&gt;1/2$, channels of the class with injective four-vertex tree functions allow consistent estimation exactly for $β&lt;2/3$, and the channels of this open set exactly for $β&lt;5/8$, at which the risk exponent drops from $1/12$ to $0$. Computer-assisted certificates show that both kinds of channels occur on open sets.</p></details> | 47 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables. Code: https://github.com/yahiko-l/pair-dependent-semantic-entropy |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. | **[A spatiotemporal Bayesian hierarchical model of heat-related mortality in Catalonia, Spain (2012--2022): The role of environmental and socioeconomic modifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17148v3)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extreme heat represents a growing threat to public health as climate change intensifies. | **[Transformer-inspired architectures make machine-learned electron densities accurate and transferable enough to bypass self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39687v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce machine learning models that accurately predict the electron density, the central variable of density functional theory. The forces not only recover the self-consistent minima in geometry optimization but also successfully drive ab initio molecular dynamics. | **[AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6$\times$ as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23$\times$ end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https://github.com/InfRec-Cardinal/AB-Attention.</p></details> | 20 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Distilling Large-Scale Machine Learning Interatomic Potentials to Predict Long-Timescale Electrolyte Transport Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39513v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurately predicting the transport properties of liquid electrolytes is critical for next-generation battery design and requires force fields that are both quantum-mechanically accurate and fast enough for long-timescale simulation. Recent large-scale pretrained machine learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) approach density-functional-theory accuracy, but remain too slow to access the necessary timescales to converge transport properties. We provide a knowledge distillation procedure to transfer the accuracy of a large-scale "teacher" MLIP into fast, system-specific "students", without any additional ab initio data. Our distilled models run ~40x faster, reproduce teacher-predicted structural observables, and produce stable 20 ns trajectories in days, rather than the months the teacher would require. Across a diverse set of sodium- and lithium-ion electrolytes, student-predicted self-diffusivities, ionic conductivities, and shear viscosities track experiment and outperform classical force-field predictions, establishing distillation as a practical route to accurate, long-timescale simulations necessary for high-throughput electrolyte screening.</p></details> |  |
| **[Challenge or Pass? A Dynamic Policy for MLB's Automated Ball-Strike System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39452v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Under Major League Baseball's automated ball-strike (ABS) challenge system, players must weigh the value of overturning a call against the risk of losing a challenge they may need later. We develop a policy that values an overturn through its effect on expected runs, accounting for the count, outs, and runners on base. Using pitch tracking and game records, we reconstruct challenge opportunities, including calls that players let stand. A Bayesian model represents players' imperfect impressions of pitch location and uses them to estimate overturn probabilities, with uncertainty calibrated from historical challenge decisions. Dynamic programming compares the expected gain with the future cost of losing a challenge, accounting for the stage of the game, the number of challenges remaining, and opportunities on both offense and defense. We develop the policy using games before the 2026 All-Star break and evaluate it without refitting on 983 games played afterward. Along the recorded sequence of opportunities, the policy recovers 0.536 modeled correction runs per game across both teams, compared with 0.452 under observed decisions, an improvement of 18.5%. The gain is 0.084 runs per game, with a 95% bootstrap interval of [0.065, 0.103]. The policy makes more challenges and recovers more value per successful review despite a lower overturn rate. These results show why overturn rate alone can be a misleading measure of challenge strategy.</p></details> | 22 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Pair-Dependent Judge Errors Change the Minimax Rate of Semantic Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39196v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Semantic entropy, the entropy of the meanings among a language model's sampled answers, is estimated from the verdicts of a judge that declares pairs of answers equivalent or not, and automatic judges err in ways that depend on the answers they compare. The existing minimax theory covers only judges whose false-equivalence probability is the same for all pairs. We model a known judge that declares two answers with meanings $a$ and $b$ equivalent with probability $q+ΔM_{ab}$, for a symmetric matrix $M$ and $q$ bounded away from $0$ and $1$, and show that for three meanings the rate is set by tree statistics on four answers, while on an open set of channels the consistency threshold is set by trees on five. For every channel in an explicit open class, the minimax squared risk is of order $N^{-1/3}$ with $N=n^4Δ^6$ on polynomial weak windows on which $n^5Δ^8$ exceeds a multiple of $\log n$, and of order $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ on every polynomial weak window when the tree functions on at most four vertices are injective. The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. For such a channel, on every polynomial weak window, the minimax risk is of the order of the sum of $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ and a term that decays exponentially in $n^5Δ^8$, so no estimator is consistent while $n^5Δ^8$ stays bounded, even as $N\to\infty$. For $Δ=n^{-β}$ with $β&gt;1/2$, channels of the class with injective four-vertex tree functions allow consistent estimation exactly for $β&lt;2/3$, and the channels of this open set exactly for $β&lt;5/8$, at which the risk exponent drops from $1/12$ to $0$. Computer-assisted certificates show that both kinds of channels occur on open sets.</p></details> | 47 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables. Code: https://github.com/yahiko-l/pair-dependent-semantic-entropy |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. Here we have $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)=2$ for $0\leq k\leq1/2$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)\leq1+k+1/(4k)$ for $k\geq1/2$, and, for real numbers $a\geq b\geq c\geq0$, $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)=a+b$ if $c(a+b)\leq ab$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)\leq(a+b+c)/2+(ab/c+ac/b+bc/a)/4$ otherwise. We moreover prove an operational barrier (which can be thought of as coming from an operational asymptotic spectrum) showing that the current analysis of all these upper bounds on $ω_\mathbb{F}$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$ is optimal and that in fact the current degeneration ingredients cannot improve the bounds.</p></details> |  |
| **[A spatiotemporal Bayesian hierarchical model of heat-related mortality in Catalonia, Spain (2012--2022): The role of environmental and socioeconomic modifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17148v3)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extreme heat represents a growing threat to public health as climate change intensifies. While its mortality impacts are well recognised, the interplay between heat, air pollution, and socioeconomic vulnerability remains insufficiently understood. We quantified the associations of extreme maximum temperatures and heatwaves with daily mortality in Catalonia (2012-2022) and evaluated how air pollutants-particularly ozone, nitrogen dioxide and particulate matter-and socioeconomic factors confound or modify this relationship. Hierarchical Bayesian zero-inflated negative binomial models were fitted with nested spatiotemporal random effects (Matérn SPDE field evolving over years; month nested within year; unstructured ABS frailty). Among 210.860 summer deaths in the complete environmental sample, extreme heat and heatwaves were associated with higher mortality after multi-pollutant and socioeconomic adjustment (e.g., extreme heat at lag 2: RR $1.105$, 95% CrI $1.086$-$1.125$; heatwave at lag 3: RR $1.142$, 95% CrI $1.116$-$1.167$; posterior Prob $\approx 1$). Ozone concentrations $\geq 100\,μ$g/m$^3$ were positively associated with mortality, and extreme heat$\times$high ozone interactions further elevated risk. Areas with higher income inequality and older population structures also showed elevated mortality. These findings indicate that heat indicators raise summer mortality in Catalonia even after environmental and socioeconomic adjustment, while compound ozone exposure and contextual vulnerability further shape risk. ## feed-forward 3D reconstruction
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[GeoCond: A Conditioning-Aware Reliability Adapter for Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18465v2)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D foundation models such as VGGT predict cameras, depth, and point maps in a single pass, but can fail silently under low overlap, low parallax, and extreme relative rotation. Stratified analyses over these factors show that these failures are governed by geometric conditioning and are poorly captured by native aleatoric confidence. We introduce GeoCond, a lightweight reliability adapter for frozen feed-forward 3D backbones. GeoCond reads geometric conditioning cues and the backbone's own stability signals and outputs pose-level uncertainty and a refinement gate. During training, it can be supervised by frame-permutation orbit variance, ground-truth pose error when labels are available, or cycle residuals from unlabelled independent pose graphs. At inference, the default head requires only one backbone pass and a small MLP. On VGGT, GeoCond improves out-of-distribution (OOD) AUSE (area under the sparsification-error curve; lower is better) from $0.32$ to $0.20$ over native confidence on held-out outdoor scenes (and to $0.21$ when trained on indoor data only) and avoids the collapse caused by applying bundle adjustment uniformly, without improving on feed-forward poses there. For three of six backbones, cycle-distilled variants provide a ground-truth-free adaptation route, including cases where permutation variance vanishes on equivariant models. The same reliability signal supports gated refinement, pose-graph weighting, calibration, curation, and capture decisions. Reliable feed-forward 3D reconstruction requires not only predicting geometry, but also knowing when that geometry should be trusted.</p></details> | Accepted in WACV 2027 |
| **[Transformer-inspired architectures make machine-learned electron densities accurate and transferable enough to bypass self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39687v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce machine learning models that accurately predict the electron density, the central variable of density functional theory. They are built on Transformer-inspired architectures, which require little computation per parameter and can therefore be enlarged at low cost. The proposed models, SwinUNETR-V2 and its attention-free counterpart NeXtUNETR, achieve state-of-the-art accuracy on QM9, with normalized mean absolute errors in the density averaging 0.075% and 0.046%, respectively. The predicted densities are accurate enough to bypass the self-consistent iteration: a single Kohn-Sham step reproduces the self-consistent electronic structure. For NeXtUNETR, the median errors are 0.036 meV in the total energy, 0.65 meV/angstrom in the forces and 1.8 meV in the HOMO-LUMO gap. The forces not only recover the self-consistent minima in geometry optimization but also successfully drive ab initio molecular dynamics. Fine-tuned on small datasets, both networks also transfer accurately to systems containing elements absent from QM9 or larger than its molecules.</p></details> | 36 pages, 4 figures, 2 tables; Extended Data (4 figures, 2 tables) and Supplementary Information included |
| **[AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6$\times$ as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23$\times$ end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https://github.com/InfRec-Cardinal/AB-Attention.</p></details> | 20 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Distilling Large-Scale Machine Learning Interatomic Potentials to Predict Long-Timescale Electrolyte Transport Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39513v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurately predicting the transport properties of liquid electrolytes is critical for next-generation battery design and requires force fields that are both quantum-mechanically accurate and fast enough for long-timescale simulation. Recent large-scale pretrained machine learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) approach density-functional-theory accuracy, but remain too slow to access the necessary timescales to converge transport properties. We provide a knowledge distillation procedure to transfer the accuracy of a large-scale "teacher" MLIP into fast, system-specific "students", without any additional ab initio data. Our distilled models run ~40x faster, reproduce teacher-predicted structural observables, and produce stable 20 ns trajectories in days, rather than the months the teacher would require. Across a diverse set of sodium- and lithium-ion electrolytes, student-predicted self-diffusivities, ionic conductivities, and shear viscosities track experiment and outperform classical force-field predictions, establishing distillation as a practical route to accurate, long-timescale simulations necessary for high-throughput electrolyte screening.</p></details> |  |
| **[Challenge or Pass? A Dynamic Policy for MLB's Automated Ball-Strike System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39452v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Under Major League Baseball's automated ball-strike (ABS) challenge system, players must weigh the value of overturning a call against the risk of losing a challenge they may need later. We develop a policy that values an overturn through its effect on expected runs, accounting for the count, outs, and runners on base. Using pitch tracking and game records, we reconstruct challenge opportunities, including calls that players let stand. A Bayesian model represents players' imperfect impressions of pitch location and uses them to estimate overturn probabilities, with uncertainty calibrated from historical challenge decisions. Dynamic programming compares the expected gain with the future cost of losing a challenge, accounting for the stage of the game, the number of challenges remaining, and opportunities on both offense and defense. We develop the policy using games before the 2026 All-Star break and evaluate it without refitting on 983 games played afterward. Along the recorded sequence of opportunities, the policy recovers 0.536 modeled correction runs per game across both teams, compared with 0.452 under observed decisions, an improvement of 18.5%. The gain is 0.084 runs per game, with a 95% bootstrap interval of [0.065, 0.103]. The policy makes more challenges and recovers more value per successful review despite a lower overturn rate. These results show why overturn rate alone can be a misleading measure of challenge strategy.</p></details> | 22 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables |
| **[Pair-Dependent Judge Errors Change the Minimax Rate of Semantic Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39196v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Semantic entropy, the entropy of the meanings among a language model's sampled answers, is estimated from the verdicts of a judge that declares pairs of answers equivalent or not, and automatic judges err in ways that depend on the answers they compare. The existing minimax theory covers only judges whose false-equivalence probability is the same for all pairs. We model a known judge that declares two answers with meanings $a$ and $b$ equivalent with probability $q+ΔM_{ab}$, for a symmetric matrix $M$ and $q$ bounded away from $0$ and $1$, and show that for three meanings the rate is set by tree statistics on four answers, while on an open set of channels the consistency threshold is set by trees on five. For every channel in an explicit open class, the minimax squared risk is of order $N^{-1/3}$ with $N=n^4Δ^6$ on polynomial weak windows on which $n^5Δ^8$ exceeds a multiple of $\log n$, and of order $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ on every polynomial weak window when the tree functions on at most four vertices are injective. The exponent comes from a cubic degeneracy at the equitable point. On an open set of channels in the class, the four-vertex tree functions take equal values at two distributions with different entropies. For such a channel, on every polynomial weak window, the minimax risk is of the order of the sum of $\min\{1,N^{-1/3}\}$ and a term that decays exponentially in $n^5Δ^8$, so no estimator is consistent while $n^5Δ^8$ stays bounded, even as $N\to\infty$. For $Δ=n^{-β}$ with $β&gt;1/2$, channels of the class with injective four-vertex tree functions allow consistent estimation exactly for $β&lt;2/3$, and the channels of this open set exactly for $β&lt;5/8$, at which the risk exponent drops from $1/12$ to $0$. Computer-assisted certificates show that both kinds of channels occur on open sets.</p></details> | 47 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables. Code: https://github.com/yahiko-l/pair-dependent-semantic-entropy |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. As modern applications transition to high-resolution imaging, there is a critical need for contour extraction that leverages multi-core CPU architectures without the complexity of tile stitching or heavy synchronization. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm designed for high-performance geometric boundary extraction. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, conducted on both consumer and server-grade CPUs, demonstrate that TRUCO significantly outperforms existing baselines. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. Event cameras report per-pixel brightness changes asynchronously at microsecond resolution, offering a complementary signal that is largely immune to these artifacts, but whether this modality can be incorporated into existing RGB based 4D reconstruction pipelines without a full architectural redesign remains an open question. In this preliminary study, we investigate a minimal extension: we augment Monst3R, an existing RGB-only 4D reconstruction model, with an additional event-camera input branch, leaving the rest of the architecture unchanged. Across 3 benchmark datasets, this simple RGB-event fusion reduces Abs Rel by 3.4% on average relative to the RGB-only Monst3R baseline, and a sensitivity analysis shows that the improvement grows with the magnitude of camera and object motion, with the largest gains observed under high object motion conditions where RGB-only reconstruction degrades most. These early results suggest that even a lightweight, non-invasive fusion of event data can meaningfully improve 4D reconstruction robustness, motivating a more thorough architectural investigation in future work.</p></details> | Accepted at NeVi 2026 workshop at the 19th European Conference on Computer Vision (ECCV 2026) |
| **[Does VGGT Know What It Does Not Know?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35806v1)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models such as VGGT regress cameras and geometry in a single forward pass, but they report no uncertainty for the camera poses they predict: an incorrect relative pose looks no different from a correct one. We ask whether the frozen model nonetheless knows when its pose is wrong. On HardMatch, a benchmark of 1,000 deliberately difficult image pairs on which VGGT fails on 35% of pairs, a linear probe on VGGT's frozen features predicts pose failure on held-out categories with ROC-AUC 93.5%, compared with 79.7% for VGGT's own point-map confidence and 77.7% for a classifier trained on all of VGGT's outputs. The probe catches failures VGGT is confident about, lets a downstream system abstain on the pairs it distrusts, and transfers without retraining to rotation failures on PhotoTourism and ScanNet-1500, beyond what co-visibility explains. This knowledge is absent from the per-image encoder: it is computed partway through the layers that fuse the two views, and the same holds for a second model, $π^3$. In essence, we find that VGGT knows more about its own failures than it reports.</p></details> | 9 pages, 6 figures, 7 tables |
| **[CoMo3R-SLAM: Collaborative Monocular Dense SLAM with Learned 3D Reconstruction Priors for Outdoor Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30488v3)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Outdoor robot teams need a shared dense map despite limited overlap, independent reference frames, and uncertain monocular scale. Collaborative dense SLAM systems typically resolve this with depth sensors, which add payload, power, and calibration cost. We present CoMo3R-SLAM, a collaborative monocular dense SLAM system that places learned feed-forward 3D reconstruction priors at the center of the multi-agent problem: their dense pointmaps anchor scale across agents and supply correspondences strong enough to verify inter-agent links geometrically. Each agent tracks and fuses its own keyframes from a single RGB stream, while a coordinator retrieves cross-agent keyframes over the prior's encoder features, verifies them by bidirectional dense pointmap matching, synchronizes the independent similarity gauges in closed form, and refines every keyframe in one unified multi-agent sim(3) graph. Finally, a pose-depth alternation over geometry-aware segments lets inter-agent observations constrain dense structure as well as trajectories. Requiring neither measured depth nor supplied intrinsics, CoMo3R-SLAM attains the lowest trajectory error on three of four Tanks and Temples scenes, and competitive accuracy on Waymo driving sequences, while running at approximately 6-8 FPS on RTX 3080 Ti. A long-horizon traversal, independently captured day and night streams, and teams of up to four agents further map its operating range.</p></details> | Code and project website: https://como3r-slam.github.io |
| **[Cryptanalysis and Repair of "Decentralized Multi-authority ABE for NC$^1$ from BDH"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29224v2)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present a plaintext-recovery attack on both the DBDH-based construction and the CBDH-based variant of Datta, Komargodski, and Waters (Journal of Cryptology, 2023). The attack succeeds with probability one in the claimed static security model using one corrupt authority and two users, each unauthorized even when granted the corrupt authority's attribute. The flaw is an invalid extension of the LSSS matrix-normalization lemma originating with Rouselakis and Waters (FC,~2015): preserving complete-share distributions does not preserve coordinate-wise ciphertext masks, which a corrupt authority can expose to remove identity binding from other users' shares. For both variants, replacing these masks with independent full linear sharings of zero preserves correctness and restores ciphertext-distribution invariance. We prove static security of both repaired variants in the random oracle model under their respective BDH assumptions, using the original hardcore-bit convention for the CBDH variant. Both proofs allow adaptive identity-hash queries. The modification retains the original matrix-width and row-labeling restrictions, keys, decryption, and ciphertext size, requiring only additional encryption randomness and finite-field arithmetic. We also attack both multi-authority attribute-based inner-product functional encryption constructions of Datta and Pal (PKC, 2023) and establish correctness and matrix-substitution invariance of the same repair. We prove static security of the repaired small-universe construction under asymmetric DBDH. For the large-universe construction, we identify a missing source-group encoding in the original reduction and give a complete static security proof under an augmented $L$-DBDH assumption, whose generic bilinear group hardness we establish.</p></details> |  |
| **[GeoLift: Grounding Video Spatial Reasoning in Explicit 3D Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33558v1)** | 2026-10-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video spatial reasoning requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to understand how objects are arranged and related across viewpoints. While these models can recognize objects in individual frames, integrating these observations to solve complex spatial tasks remains challenging. We examine route planning as a representative task and observe that existing models become substantially less accurate as the number of required turns increases, with several models achieving accuracy close to random guessing on routes requiring three or more turns. This finding suggests that recognizing landmarks alone is insufficient: models must also connect observations across viewpoints and track how landmarks are arranged in a shared space. Motivated by this observation, we introduce GeoLift, a training-free framework that uses feed-forward 3D reconstruction to map objects detected in individual frames into a shared metric 3D scene. GeoLift combines a frozen MLLM for 2D grounding with pretrained geometry models for scene reconstruction. It then uses shared geometric regions, called superpoints, to associate observations of the same object across views without a learned matcher. The reconstructed 3D map enables explicit computation of spatial relations and multistep routes, and provides geometric evidence for estimating distances and sizes. With all model weights frozen, GeoLift improves Qwen3-VL-4B's average score on VSI-Bench from 55.4% to 64.1%, including an increase in route-planning accuracy from 30.9% to 53.6%, and achieves 59.8% accuracy on MindCube. These results show that connecting frame-level object grounding through a shared 3D map can improve complex spatial reasoning without task-specific training.</p></details> | 11 pages, 3 figures, 4 tables. Xinglin Li and TingTing Long contributed equally. Jin Xie is the corresponding author |
| **[PixGR: Pixel-Space Generative Decoding for Fast Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31908v1)** | 2026-10-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present PixGR, an efficient framework that integrates lightweight pixel-space generative modeling into feed-forward 3D Gaussian reconstruction to model scene uncertainty. Experiments demonstrate that PixGR outperforms regression-based approaches in challenging sparse-view settings, particularly with widely spaced observations and view extrapolation. Meanwhile, it maintains sub-second inference and remains competitive with substantially heavier pretrained generative models. Our project page is available at https://ranrhuang.github.io/pixgr/.</p></details> | Project page: https://ranrhuang.github.io/pixgr/ |

| **[GeoGSplat: Geometry-Grounded Non-Pixel-Aligned Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36760v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward Gaussian Splatting enables efficient novel view synthesis, but pixel-aligned prediction makes the primitive count grow with image resolution and view count, leading to substantial redundancy and increased computational cost. | **[Multiplying matrices via polynomials: exposition and barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30054v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We give an exposition of OpenAI's recent proof that the exponent $ω_\mathbb{C}$ of matrix multiplication over the complex numbers satisfies $ω_\mathbb{C}\leq9/4$. | **[A spatiotemporal Bayesian hierarchical model of heat-related mortality in Catalonia, Spain (2012--2022): The role of environmental and socioeconomic modifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17148v3)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extreme heat represents a growing threat to public health as climate change intensifies.
